<p><b>Alexei Nikolaevich</b> (Russian: <span lang="ru">Алексе́й Никола́евич</span>) (12 August [O.S. 30 July] 1904 – 17 July 1918) was the last <a href="/wiki/Tsesarevich" title="Tsesarevich">Tsesarevich</a> (<a href="/wiki/Heir_apparent" title="Heir apparent">heir apparent</a> to the throne of the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Empire" title="Russian Empire">Russian Empire</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;note 1&#93;</a></sup> He was the youngest child and only son of <a href="/wiki/List_of_Russian_rulers" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Russian rulers">Emperor</a> <a href="/wiki/Nicholas_II_of_Russia" title="Nicholas II of Russia">Nicholas II</a> and <a href="/wiki/Empress_of_Russia" class="mw-redirect" title="Empress of Russia">Empress</a> <a href="/wiki/Alexandra_Feodorovna_(Alix_of_Hesse)" title="Alexandra Feodorovna (Alix of Hesse)">Alexandra Feodorovna</a>. He was born with <a href="/wiki/Haemophilia" title="Haemophilia">haemophilia</a>, which his parents tried treating with the methods of a peasant <a href="/wiki/Faith_healer" class="mw-redirect" title="Faith healer">faith healer</a> named <a href="/wiki/Grigori_Rasputin" title="Grigori Rasputin">Grigori Rasputin</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After the <a href="/wiki/February_Revolution" title="February Revolution">February Revolution</a> of 1917, the Romanovs were sent into internal exile in <a href="/wiki/Tobolsk" title="Tobolsk">Tobolsk</a>, <a href="/wiki/Siberia" title="Siberia">Siberia</a>. After the <a href="/wiki/October_Revolution" title="October Revolution">October Revolution</a>, the family was initially to be tried in a court of law, before the intensification of the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Civil_War" title="Russian Civil War">Russian Civil War</a> made execution increasingly favorable in the eyes of the Soviet government. With <a href="/wiki/White_movement" title="White movement">White Army</a> soldiers rapidly approaching, the Ural Regional Soviet ordered the <a href="/wiki/Murder_of_the_Romanov_family" title="Murder of the Romanov family">murder</a> of Alexei, the rest of his family, and four remaining retainers on 17 July 1918. Rumors persisted for decades that Alexei had escaped his execution, with multiple <a href="/wiki/Romanov_impostors#Alexei_impostors" title="Romanov impostors">impostors</a> claiming his identity. Alexei's remains, along with those of his sister Maria (or Anastasia), were ultimately discovered in a secondary grave near the rest of the Romanov family in 2007. On 17 July 1998, the 80th anniversary of their execution, Alexei's parents, three of his sisters, and the four retainers, were formally interred in the Cathedral of St. Peter and Paul, while Alexei's and Maria's (or Anastasia's) bones remain in Russian state archives. The Romanov family was <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonized</a> as <a href="/wiki/Passion_bearer" title="Passion bearer">passion bearers</a> by the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church" title="Russian Orthodox Church">Russian Orthodox Church</a> in 2000.
</p><p>Alexei is sometimes known to Russian legitimists as <b>Alexei II</b> after his ancestor <a href="/wiki/Alexis_of_Russia" title="Alexis of Russia">Alexis of Russia</a>, as until his death they do not recognize the abdication of his father in favor of his uncle <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duke_Michael_Alexandrovich_of_Russia" title="Grand Duke Michael Alexandrovich of Russia">Grand Duke Michael</a> as lawful.<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Appearance_and_personality">Appearance and personality</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexei_Nikolaevich,_Tsarevich_of_Russia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Appearance and personality">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Alexei was a handsome boy, and he bore a striking resemblance to his mother. His tutor <a href="/wiki/Pierre_Gilliard" title="Pierre Gilliard">Pierre Gilliard</a> described the 18-month-old Alexei as "one of the handsomest babies one could imagine, with his lovely fair curls and his great blue-grey eyes under their fringe of long curling lashes".<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> A few years later, Gilliard described Alexei as tall for his age, with "a long, finely chiseled face, delicate features, auburn hair with a coppery glint, and large grey-blue eyes like his mother".<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Baroness <a href="/wiki/Sophie_Buxhoeveden" title="Sophie Buxhoeveden">Sophie Buxhoeveden</a>, his mother's lady-in-waiting, reflected that "he was a pretty child, tall for his age, with regular features, splendid dark blue eyes with a spark of mischief in them, brown hair, and an upright figure".<sup id="cite_ref-:0_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexei was proud of and enjoyed his position as tsarevich. Buxhoeveden reflected that "he knew and felt that he was the Tsarevich, and from babyhood mechanically took his place in front of his elder sisters".<sup id="cite_ref-:0_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> He liked being kissed on the hand by the officers and "didn't miss his chance to boast about it and give himself airs in front of his sisters".<sup id="cite_ref-Helen_Rappaport_p._109_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Helen_Rappaport_p._109-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> He enjoyed jumping in front of the guards at the front of the Alexander Palace, who would immediately salute him as he walked past. Nicholas forbade the guards to salute Alexei unless another member of the family accompanied him. Alexei was embarrassed "when the salute failed him", which "marked his first taste of discipline".<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> On one occasion, he ordered all of the Finnish officers on various ships to stand before him on the deck of the <i>Standart</i>. He began commanding them, but the Finnish officers did not understand Russian and stood in confusion until an aide informed them that Alexei wanted to hear them say, "We wish you health, your Imperial Highness."<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> When he was told that a group of officers had arrived to call on him, the 6-year-old Alexei told his sisters, "Now girls, run away. I am busy. Someone has just called to see me on business."<sup id="cite_ref-Robert_K._Massie_p._139_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Robert_K._Massie_p._139-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> Also at 6, he walked into his father's study and saw the Foreign Minister <a href="/wiki/Alexander_Izvolsky" title="Alexander Izvolsky">Alexander Izvolsky</a> waiting to see Nicholas. Izvolsky remained sitting, and Alexei screamed, "When the heir to the Russian Throne enters a room, people must get up!"<sup id="cite_ref-Robert_K._Massie_p._139_12-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Robert_K._Massie_p._139-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexei's parents indulged his tantrums and mischievous behavior, and they rarely punished him. In 1906, Alexei and his family went on a cruise to Finland. In the middle of the night, the 2-year-old Alexei commanded the ship's band to wake up and play for him.<sup id="cite_ref-Helen_Rappaport_p._109_9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Helen_Rappaport_p._109-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> Instead of punishing Alexei, Nicholas joked "that's the way to bring up an autocrat!"<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> Nicholas called Alexei "Alexei the Terrible."<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexei loved to attend army inspections with his father. When he was 3, he wore a miniature army uniform and played with a toy wooden rifle.<sup id="cite_ref-Helen_Rappaport_p._109_9-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Helen_Rappaport_p._109-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> From birth, he had the title of "Hetman of all the Cossacks."<sup id="cite_ref-Robert_K._Massie_p._140_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Robert_K._Massie_p._140-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> He wore a miniature uniform of a sailor of the Russian navy, and he had his own Cossack uniform with a fur cap, boots, and dagger.<sup id="cite_ref-Robert_K._Massie_p._140_15-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Robert_K._Massie_p._140-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> He ended his daily prayers with "Hurrah!" instead of "amen". When asked why, he replied that the soldiers on parade always said "Hurrah!" when his father finished speaking, so he should greet his Heavenly Father in the same way.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Before he understood the nature of his disease, he said that he wanted to be a warrior-tsar and lead armies as his ancestors had.<sup id="cite_ref-Robert_K._Massie_p._140_15-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Robert_K._Massie_p._140-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexei resented that he was forbidden from doing what other boys could do because of his hemophilia. When his mother forbade him to ride a bike and play tennis, he asked angrily, "Why can other boys have everything and I nothing?"<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> All four of his sisters were accomplished horsewomen, but he was forbidden from horseriding.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexei had few friends his age and was often lonely. Alexandra did not allow Alexei to play with his Romanov cousins because she was worried that they would knock him down when playing and he might bleed.<sup id="cite_ref-Robert_K._Massie_p._142_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Robert_K._Massie_p._142-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> Alexei's companions were his sailor-nanny Derevenko's two young sons. Derevenko watched them as they played, and he chastised his children if they played too roughly with Alexei.<sup id="cite_ref-Robert_K._Massie_p._142_19-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Robert_K._Massie_p._142-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexei was close with his sisters. Gilliard wrote that they "brought into his life an element of youthful merriment that otherwise would have been sorely missed".<sup id="cite_ref-Robert_K._Massie_p._142_19-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Robert_K._Massie_p._142-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Despite the hemophilia, Alexei was adventurous and restless. Doctor <a href="/wiki/Eugene_Botkin" title="Eugene Botkin">Eugene Botkin</a>'s children noticed Alexei's inability to "stay in any place or at any game for any length of time".<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> When he was 7, he stole a bicycle and rode it around the palace. Shocked, Nicholas ordered every guard to pursue and capture Alexei.<sup id="cite_ref-Robert_K._Massie_p._140_15-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Robert_K._Massie_p._140-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> At a children's party, Alexei began jumping from table to table. When Derevenko tried to stop him, Alexei shouted, "All grown-ups have to go!"<sup id="cite_ref-Robert_K._Massie_p._140_15-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Robert_K._Massie_p._140-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Recognizing Alexei's energetic nature, Nicholas ordered that Alexei be allowed "to do everything that other children of his age were wont to do, and not to restrain him unless it was absolutely necessary".<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexei was disobedient and difficult to control. Olga could not manage Alexei's "peevish temper".<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> The only person he obeyed was his father. <a href="/wiki/Sydney_Gibbes" title="Sydney Gibbes">Sydney Gibbes</a> noted that "one word [from Nicholas] was always enough to exact implicit obedience from [Alexei]".<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> Buxhoeveden remembered that Alexei had once thrown her parasol in the river, and Nicholas had chastised Alexei: "That is not the way for a gentleman to behave to a lady. I am ashamed of you, Alexei."<sup id="cite_ref-Sophie_Buxhoeveden_p._311_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sophie_Buxhoeveden_p._311-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> After his father scolded him, Alexei was "scarlet in the face" and apologized to Sophie.<sup id="cite_ref-Sophie_Buxhoeveden_p._311_24-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sophie_Buxhoeveden_p._311-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As a small child, Alexei occasionally played pranks on guests. At a formal dinner party, Alexei removed the shoe of a female guest from under the table, and showed it to his father. Nicholas sternly told the boy to return the "trophy", which Alexei did after placing a large ripe strawberry into the toe of the shoe.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As he grew up, Alexei became more thoughtful and considerate. When he was 9, he sent a collection of his favorite jingles to <a href="/wiki/Gleb_Botkin" title="Gleb Botkin">Gleb Botkin</a>, Eugene Botkin's son. He asked Gleb, who was talented at drawing, to illustrate the jingles. He attached a note: "To illustrate and write the jingles under the drawings. Alexei." Before handing the note to Eugene Botkin, he crossed out his signature and explained, "If I send that paper to Gleb with my signature on it, then it would be an order which Gleb would have to obey. But I mean it only as a request and he doesn't have to do it if he doesn't want to."<sup id="cite_ref-Robert_K._Massie_p._139_12-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Robert_K._Massie_p._139-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexei enjoyed playing the <a href="/wiki/Balalaika" title="Balalaika">balalaika</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Robert_K._Massie_p._140_15-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Robert_K._Massie_p._140-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexei's favorite pet was a spaniel named Joy.<sup id="cite_ref-Robert_K._Massie_p._140_15-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Robert_K._Massie_p._140-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Nicholas gave Alexei an old performing donkey named Vanka. Alexei gave sugar cubes to Vanka, and Vanka pulled Alexei around the park in a sled during the winter.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to Gilliard, Alexei was a simple, affectionate child, but the court spoiled him by the "servile flattery" of the servants and "silly adulations" of the people around him. Once, a deputation of peasants came to bring presents to Alexei. Derevenko required that they kneel before Alexei. Gilliard remarked that the Tsarevich was "embarrassed and blushed violently", and when asked if he liked seeing people on their knees before him, he said, "Oh no, but Derevenko says it must be so!". When Gilliard encouraged Alexei to "stop Derevenko insisting on it", he said that he "dare not". When Gilliard took the matter up with Derevenko, he said that Alexei was "delighted to be freed from this irksome formality".<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>"Alexei was the center of this united family, the focus of all its hopes and affections", wrote Gilliard. "His sisters worshipped him. He was his parents' pride and joy. When he was well, the palace was transformed. Everyone and everything in it seemed bathed in sunshine."<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Gilliard eventually convinced Alexei's parents that granting the boy greater autonomy would help him develop better self-control. Alexei took advantage of his unaccustomed freedom, and began to outgrow some of his earlier foibles.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> Courtiers reported that his illness made him sensitive to the hurts of others.<sup id="cite_ref-Massie,_pp._136-146_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Massie,_pp._136-146-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Due to his disease, Alexei understood that he might not live to adulthood. When he was ten, his older sister Olga found him lying on his back looking at the clouds and asked him what he was doing. "I like to think and wonder", Alexei replied. Olga asked him what he liked to think about. "Oh, so many things", the boy responded. "I enjoy the sun and the beauty of summer as long as I can. Who knows whether one of these days I shall not be prevented from doing it?"<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>
Nicholas' Colonel Mordinov remembered Alexei: <style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style></p><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>He had what we Russians usually call "a golden heart". He easily felt an attachment to people, he liked them and tried to do his best to help them, especially when it seemed to him that someone was unjustly hurt. His love, like that of his parents, was based mainly on pity. Tsarevich Alexei Nikolaevich was an awfully lazy, but very capable boy (I think, he was lazy precisely because he was capable), he easily grasped everything, he was thoughtful and keen beyond his years ... Despite his good nature and compassion, he undoubtedly promised to possess a firm and independent character in the future.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_years">Early years</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexei_Nikolaevich,_Tsarevich_of_Russia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Early years">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Alexei_Nikolaevich,_1904.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Alexei_Nikolaevich%2C_1904.jpg/220px-Alexei_Nikolaevich%2C_1904.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="220" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Alexei_Nikolaevich%2C_1904.jpg/330px-Alexei_Nikolaevich%2C_1904.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Alexei_Nikolaevich%2C_1904.jpg/440px-Alexei_Nikolaevich%2C_1904.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3192" data-file-height="3192" /></a><figcaption>Alexei as an infant in 1904</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tsesarevich_Alexei_(right)_with_his_sailor_nanny_Andrei_Dereven%27ko_aboard_the_Imperial_yacht_Standart_on_June_1908.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Tsesarevich_Alexei_%28right%29_with_his_sailor_nanny_Andrei_Dereven%27ko_aboard_the_Imperial_yacht_Standart_on_June_1908.jpg/220px-Tsesarevich_Alexei_%28right%29_with_his_sailor_nanny_Andrei_Dereven%27ko_aboard_the_Imperial_yacht_Standart_on_June_1908.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="268" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Tsesarevich_Alexei_%28right%29_with_his_sailor_nanny_Andrei_Dereven%27ko_aboard_the_Imperial_yacht_Standart_on_June_1908.jpg/330px-Tsesarevich_Alexei_%28right%29_with_his_sailor_nanny_Andrei_Dereven%27ko_aboard_the_Imperial_yacht_Standart_on_June_1908.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Tsesarevich_Alexei_%28right%29_with_his_sailor_nanny_Andrei_Dereven%27ko_aboard_the_Imperial_yacht_Standart_on_June_1908.jpg/440px-Tsesarevich_Alexei_%28right%29_with_his_sailor_nanny_Andrei_Dereven%27ko_aboard_the_Imperial_yacht_Standart_on_June_1908.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1644" data-file-height="2000" /></a><figcaption>Alexei (right) with his sailor nanny Andrei Derevenko aboard the Imperial yacht <i><a href="/wiki/Standart_(yacht)" class="mw-redirect" title="Standart (yacht)">Standart</a></i> in 1908</figcaption></figure>
<p>Alexei was born on 12 August&#160;&#91;<a href="/wiki/Old_Style_and_New_Style_dates" title="Old Style and New Style dates">O.S.</a> 30 July&#93;&#160;1904 in <a href="/wiki/Peterhof_Palace" title="Peterhof Palace">Peterhof Palace</a>, <a href="/wiki/Saint_Petersburg_Governorate" title="Saint Petersburg Governorate">St. Petersburg Governorate</a>, <a href="/wiki/Russian_Empire" title="Russian Empire">Russian Empire</a>. He was the youngest of five children of Emperor <a href="/wiki/Nicholas_II_of_Russia" title="Nicholas II of Russia">Nicholas II</a> and Empress <a href="/wiki/Alexandra_Feodorovna_(Alix_of_Hesse)" title="Alexandra Feodorovna (Alix of Hesse)">Alexandra Feodorovna</a>. His father was the eldest son of <a href="/wiki/Alexander_III_of_Russia" title="Alexander III of Russia">Emperor Alexander III</a> and <a href="/wiki/Maria_Feodorovna_(Dagmar_of_Denmark)" title="Maria Feodorovna (Dagmar of Denmark)">Princess Dagmar of Denmark</a>, and his mother was the sixth child of <a href="/wiki/Louis_IV,_Grand_Duke_of_Hesse" title="Louis IV, Grand Duke of Hesse">Louis IV, Grand Duke of Hesse</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Princess_Alice_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Princess Alice of the United Kingdom">Princess Alice of the United Kingdom</a>. His paternal great-grandparents were <a href="/wiki/Alexander_II_of_Russia" title="Alexander II of Russia">Alexander II of Russia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Christian_IX_of_Denmark" title="Christian IX of Denmark">Christian IX of Denmark</a> and <a href="/wiki/Louise_of_Hesse-Kassel" title="Louise of Hesse-Kassel">Louise of Hesse-Kassel</a>, while his maternal great-grandparents were <a href="/wiki/Queen_Victoria" title="Queen Victoria">Queen Victoria</a> and her husband <a href="/wiki/Albert,_Prince_Consort" class="mw-redirect" title="Albert, Prince Consort">Albert, Prince Consort</a>. His older sisters were the Grand Duchesses <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchess_Olga_Nikolaevna_of_Russia" title="Grand Duchess Olga Nikolaevna of Russia">Olga</a>, <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchess_Tatiana_Nikolaevna_of_Russia" title="Grand Duchess Tatiana Nikolaevna of Russia">Tatiana</a>, <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchess_Maria_Nikolaevna_of_Russia" title="Grand Duchess Maria Nikolaevna of Russia">Maria</a> and <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchess_Anastasia_Nikolaevna_of_Russia" title="Grand Duchess Anastasia Nikolaevna of Russia">Anastasia</a>.
</p><p>Alexei's birth was greeted with widespread joy, because he was the first son Nicholas had had after four daughters. When she woke up from the <a href="/wiki/Chloroform" title="Chloroform">chloroform</a>, Alexandra saw the happy faces around her and exclaimed, "Oh, it cannot be true. It cannot be true. Is it really a boy?"<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> Nicholas wrote in his diary that today was "a great and "unforgettable day for us . . . there are no words to thank God enough for sending us this comfort in a time of sore trials."<sup id="cite_ref-Simon_Sebag_Montefiore_p._518_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Simon_Sebag_Montefiore_p._518-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> According to <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duke_Konstantin_Konstantinovich_of_Russia" title="Grand Duke Konstantin Konstantinovich of Russia">Grand Duke Constantine Constantinovich of Russia</a>, Nicholas' younger brother <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duke_Michael_Alexandrovich_of_Russia" title="Grand Duke Michael Alexandrovich of Russia">Grand Duke Michael Alexandrovich</a> "was radiant with happiness at no longer being heir."<sup id="cite_ref-Simon_Sebag_Montefiore_p._518_34-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Simon_Sebag_Montefiore_p._518-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> Baroness <a href="/wiki/Sophie_Buxhoeveden" title="Sophie Buxhoeveden">Sophie Buxhoeveden</a> remembered that "we were nearly deafened by the church bells ringing all day".<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> St. Petersburg "was ablaze with flags" and "the people gave themselves over to public rejoicing."<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> Nicholas granted political amnesty to prisoners and set up a fund for military and naval scholarships.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Nicholas named Alexei after <a href="/wiki/Alexis_of_Russia" title="Alexis of Russia">Alexis of Russia</a>, his favorite emperor.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> His doting family called him "Baby." He was later also affectionately referred to as Alyosha (Алёша).
</p><p>As soon as he was born, Alexei was granted the title of <a href="/wiki/Tsarevich" title="Tsarevich">tsarevich</a> and heir apparent to the Imperial Throne of Russia. An official announcement read, "From now on, in accordance with the Fundamental Laws of the Empire, the Imperial title of Heir Tsarevich, and all the rights pertaining to it, belong to Our Son Alexei.".<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Alexei was christened on 3 September 1904 in the chapel in <a href="/wiki/Peterhof_Palace" title="Peterhof Palace">Peterhof Palace</a>. His principal godparents were his paternal <a href="/wiki/Maria_Feodorovna_(Dagmar_of_Denmark)" title="Maria Feodorovna (Dagmar of Denmark)">grandmother</a> and his great-uncle, <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duke_Alexei_Alexandrovich_of_Russia" title="Grand Duke Alexei Alexandrovich of Russia">Grand Duke Alexei Alexandrovich</a>. His other godparents included his oldest sister, Olga; his great-grandfather <a href="/wiki/Christian_IX_of_Denmark" title="Christian IX of Denmark">King Christian IX of Denmark</a>; <a href="/wiki/Edward_VII" title="Edward VII">King Edward VII of the United Kingdom</a>, the <a href="/wiki/George_V_of_the_United_Kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="George V of the United Kingdom">Prince of Wales</a> and <a href="/wiki/Wilhelm_II,_German_Emperor" title="Wilhelm II, German Emperor">Wilhelm II, German Emperor</a>. As Russia was at <a href="/wiki/Russo-Japanese_War" title="Russo-Japanese War">war with Japan</a>, all active soldiers and officers of the Russian Army and Navy were named honorary godfathers.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources"><span title="This citation requires a reference to the specific page or range of pages in which the material appears. (August 2020)">page&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The christening marked the first time that some of the younger members of the Imperial Family, including some of the younger sons of <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duke_Konstantin_Konstantinovich_of_Russia" title="Grand Duke Konstantin Konstantinovich of Russia">Grand Duke Konstantin Konstantinovich</a>; the Grand Duchesses Olga and Tatiana; and their cousin <a href="/wiki/Princess_Irina_Alexandrovna_of_Russia" title="Princess Irina Alexandrovna of Russia">Princess Irina Alexandrovna</a>, attended an official ceremony. For the occasion, the boys wore miniature military uniforms, and the girls wore smaller versions of the court dress and little <a href="/wiki/Kokoshnik" title="Kokoshnik">kokoshniks</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (November 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The sermon was delivered by <a href="/wiki/John_of_Kronstadt" title="John of Kronstadt">John of Kronstadt</a>. The baby was carried to the font by the elderly Princess <a href="/wiki/Maria_Golitzyna" title="Maria Golitzyna">Maria Mikhailovna Galitzine</a>, Mistress of the Robes. As a precaution, she had rubber soles put on her shoes to prevent her slipping and dropping him.
</p><p>Countess <a href="/wiki/Sophie_Buxhoeveden" title="Sophie Buxhoeveden">Sophie Buxhoeveden</a> recalled:<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>The baby lay on a pillow of cloth of gold, slung to the Princess's shoulders by a broad gold band. He was covered with the heavy cloth-of-gold mantle, lined with <a href="/wiki/Stoat" title="Stoat">ermine</a>, worn by the heir to the crown. The mantle was supported on one side by Prince Alexander Sergeiovich Dolgorouky, the Grand Marshal of the Court, and on the other by Count [Paul] Benckendorff, as decreed by custom and wise precaution. The baby wept loudly, as might any ordinary baby when old Father Yanishev dipped him in the font. His four small sisters, in short Court dresses, gazed open-eyed at the ceremony, Olga Nicholaevna, then nine years old, being in the important position of one of the godmothers. According to Russian custom, the Emperor and Empress were not present at the baptism, but directly after the ceremony, the Emperor went to the church. Both he and the Empress always confessed to feeling very nervous on these occasions, for fear that the Princess might slip, or that Father Yanishev, who was very old, might drop the baby in the font.</p></blockquote>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Hemophilia">Hemophilia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexei_Nikolaevich,_Tsarevich_of_Russia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Hemophilia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Haemophilia_in_European_royalty" title="Haemophilia in European royalty">Haemophilia in European royalty</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Palace_below.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Palace_below.jpg/220px-Palace_below.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="154" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Palace_below.jpg/330px-Palace_below.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Palace_below.jpg/440px-Palace_below.jpg 2x" data-file-width="900" data-file-height="632" /></a><figcaption>The former palace of Russian emperors in the Polish <a href="/wiki/Bia%C5%82owie%C5%BCa_Forest" title="Białowieża Forest">Białowieża Forest</a>, where Alexei had a particularly grave crisis, early October 1912</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:%D0%A0%D0%BE%D1%81%D1%82%D0%BE%D0%B2_300%D0%A0%D0%BE%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BE%D0%B2%D1%8B.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/%D0%A0%D0%BE%D1%81%D1%82%D0%BE%D0%B2_300%D0%A0%D0%BE%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BE%D0%B2%D1%8B.jpg/220px-%D0%A0%D0%BE%D1%81%D1%82%D0%BE%D0%B2_300%D0%A0%D0%BE%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BE%D0%B2%D1%8B.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="130" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/%D0%A0%D0%BE%D1%81%D1%82%D0%BE%D0%B2_300%D0%A0%D0%BE%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BE%D0%B2%D1%8B.jpg/330px-%D0%A0%D0%BE%D1%81%D1%82%D0%BE%D0%B2_300%D0%A0%D0%BE%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BE%D0%B2%D1%8B.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/%D0%A0%D0%BE%D1%81%D1%82%D0%BE%D0%B2_300%D0%A0%D0%BE%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BE%D0%B2%D1%8B.jpg/440px-%D0%A0%D0%BE%D1%81%D1%82%D0%BE%D0%B2_300%D0%A0%D0%BE%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BE%D0%B2%D1%8B.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="471" /></a><figcaption>The Imperial family in 1913, in <a href="/wiki/Rostov" title="Rostov">Rostov</a></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Pierre_Gilliard_and_Alexei.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/Pierre_Gilliard_and_Alexei.jpg/220px-Pierre_Gilliard_and_Alexei.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/Pierre_Gilliard_and_Alexei.jpg/330px-Pierre_Gilliard_and_Alexei.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/Pierre_Gilliard_and_Alexei.jpg/440px-Pierre_Gilliard_and_Alexei.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1360" /></a><figcaption>Pierre Gilliard and Alexei</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Alexei_Nikolaievich_of_Russia_and_boatswain_Derevenko.jpeg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Alexei_Nikolaievich_of_Russia_and_boatswain_Derevenko.jpeg/220px-Alexei_Nikolaievich_of_Russia_and_boatswain_Derevenko.jpeg" decoding="async" width="220" height="334" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Alexei_Nikolaievich_of_Russia_and_boatswain_Derevenko.jpeg/330px-Alexei_Nikolaievich_of_Russia_and_boatswain_Derevenko.jpeg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Alexei_Nikolaievich_of_Russia_and_boatswain_Derevenko.jpeg/440px-Alexei_Nikolaievich_of_Russia_and_boatswain_Derevenko.jpeg 2x" data-file-width="2118" data-file-height="3217" /></a><figcaption>Alexei and boatswain Derevenko</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Sankt-Peterburg_oldfoto_13645.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Sankt-Peterburg_oldfoto_13645.jpg/220px-Sankt-Peterburg_oldfoto_13645.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="228" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Sankt-Peterburg_oldfoto_13645.jpg/330px-Sankt-Peterburg_oldfoto_13645.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Sankt-Peterburg_oldfoto_13645.jpg/440px-Sankt-Peterburg_oldfoto_13645.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1840" data-file-height="1904" /></a><figcaption>Nicholas II rows his son on a boat in the Alexander Park in 1911</figcaption></figure>
<p>Alexei inherited <a href="/wiki/Haemophilia" title="Haemophilia">hemophilia</a> from his mother Alexandra, an X chromosome hereditary condition that typically affects males, which she had acquired through the line of her maternal grandmother <a href="/wiki/Queen_Victoria" title="Queen Victoria">Queen Victoria of the United Kingdom</a>. It was known as the "Royal Disease" because so many descendants of the intermarried European royal families had it (or carried it, in the case of females.) In 2009, genetic analysis determined that Alexei had <a href="/wiki/Hemophilia_B" class="mw-redirect" title="Hemophilia B">hemophilia B</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>At first, Alexei seemed like a healthy, ordinary baby. He weighed 11 pounds at birth. His paternal aunt Xenia wrote that "he's an amazingly hefty baby with a chest like a barrel and generally has the air of a warrior knight."<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> After his <a href="/wiki/Umbilical_cord" title="Umbilical cord">umbilical cord</a> was cut, his navel continued to bleed for hours and his blood did not clot. Nicholas wrote that Alexei lost "1/8 to 1/9 of the total quantity" of his blood in 48 hours.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> Nicholas reflected in his diary that "Alix and I were very alarmed by the bleeding of young Alexei that came at intervals from his umbilical cord until evening. How painful it is to experience such anxieties!"<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Nicholas and Alexandra decided not to reveal what Alexei's disease was to the Russian public or even to family members. After Alexei's first bleeding attack, Alexandra wrote, "Oh, what anguish it was... not to let others see the knife digging in one."<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> According to his French tutor, <a href="/wiki/Pierre_Gilliard" title="Pierre Gilliard">Pierre Gilliard</a>, the nature of his illness was kept a state secret.
</p><p>There were rumors about what Alexei's disease was, because of the secrecy around it. Guesses ranged from a "certain form of infantile <a href="/wiki/Tuberculosis" title="Tuberculosis">tuberculosis</a> which gives rise to acute alarm" to "one of the layers of his skin was missing."<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> An American magazine ascribed Alexei's "ill health" to "the misfortune that so many residences of the Tzars leave much to be desired from the point of view of sanitary science."<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> After Alexei's life-threatening hemorrhage at Spala, the American press speculated that Alexei had been stabbed "during an unguarded moment" by a "<a href="/wiki/Russian_nihilist_movement" title="Russian nihilist movement">nihilist</a>."<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> <i>The Times</i> observed that the "incomprehensible silence of the Court bulletins" gave "free scope to the sensation-mongers."<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>When he was 5, Alexei was assigned two navy sailors who monitored him at all times to ensure that he wouldn't injure himself. His parents appointed two sailors from the Imperial Navy: Petty Officer Andrei Derevenko and his assistant Seaman <a href="/wiki/Klimenty_Grigorievich_Nagorny" title="Klimenty Grigorievich Nagorny">Klementy Nagorny</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Anna_Vyrubova" title="Anna Vyrubova">Anna Vyrubova</a>, Alexandra's friend, remembered that "Derevenko was so patient and resourceful, that he often did wonders in alleviating the pain. I can still hear the plaintive voice of Alexis begging the big sailor, 'Lift my arm,' 'Put up my leg,' 'Warm my hands,' and I can see the patient, calm-eyed man working for hours to give comfort to the little pain-wracked limbs."<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>His hemophilia was so severe that trivial injuries such as a bruise, a nosebleed or a cut were potentially life-threatening. His parents constantly worried about him. In addition, the recurring episodes of illness and long recoveries interfered greatly with Alexei's childhood and education.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Nicholas and Alexandra believed that <a href="/wiki/Grigori_Rasputin" title="Grigori Rasputin">Grigori Rasputin</a>, a peasant monk, had the power to heal Alexei. Due to the secrecy around Alexei's condition, there are few records about Alexei's attacks and how often Rasputin treated him.
</p><p>In the autumn of 1907, Alexei fell and hurt his leg when he was playing in the Alexander Park. The fall triggered an internal hemorrhage.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> His paternal aunt <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchess_Olga_Alexandrovna_of_Russia" title="Grand Duchess Olga Alexandrovna of Russia">Grand Duchess Olga Alexandrovna of Russia</a> reflected that "the poor child lay in such pain, dark patches under his eyes and his little body all distorted, and the leg terribly swollen."<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> The doctors could do nothing. Alexandra telegraphed <a href="/wiki/Princess_Anastasia_of_Montenegro" title="Princess Anastasia of Montenegro">Princess Anastasia of Montenegro</a> and asked her to find Rasputin. Rasputin prayed over Alexei and told Alexei "Your pain is going away. You will soon be well. You must thank God for healing you. And now, go to sleep."<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> Soon after Rasputin left, the swelling in Alexei's leg went down. <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchess_Olga_Alexandrovna_of_Russia" title="Grand Duchess Olga Alexandrovna of Russia">Grand Duchess Olga Alexandrovna of Russia</a> was amazed by Alexei's swift recovery, and she wrote that the next morning Alexei "was not just alive – but well. He was sitting up in bed, the fever gone, the eyes clear and bright, not a sign of any swelling on his leg."<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In September 1912, Alexei and his family visited their hunting retreat in the <a href="/wiki/Bia%C5%82owie%C5%BCa_Forest" title="Białowieża Forest">Białowieża Forest</a>. On 5 September Alexei jumped into a rowboat and hit his groin on the oarlocks. A large bruise appeared within minutes but in a week reduced in size.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> In mid-September, the family moved to <a href="/wiki/Spa%C5%82a" title="Spała">Spała</a> (then in <a href="/wiki/Russian_Poland" class="mw-redirect" title="Russian Poland">Russian Poland</a>). On 2 October, Alexei accompanied his mother on a drive in the woods. The "juddering of the carriage had caused still healing hematoma in his upper thigh to rupture and start bleeding again."<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> He had to be carried out of the carriage in an almost unconscious state. His temperature rose, his heartbeat dropped, and he hemorrhaged in his upper thigh and abdomen. For 11 days, he screamed "O Lord have mercy on me!" and begged Alexandra, "Mama, help me!"<sup id="cite_ref-Simon_Sebag_Montefiore_p._555_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Simon_Sebag_Montefiore_p._555-61">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> He asked Alexandra to "build me a little monument of stones in the woods" and asked "When I'm dead, it won't hurt any more, will it?"<sup id="cite_ref-Simon_Sebag_Montefiore_p._555_61-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Simon_Sebag_Montefiore_p._555-61">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> Alexandra never left his bedside, and she refused to rest or eat. Nicholas "took turns with Alix to sit with Alexei," but he "rushed weeping bitterly to his study" when he saw Alexei scream.<sup id="cite_ref-Simon_Sebag_Montefiore_p._555_61-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Simon_Sebag_Montefiore_p._555-61">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> On 6 October, Alexei's fever rose to 39 degrees and he hemorrhaged into his stomach. On 8 October, Alexei received the <a href="/wiki/Anointing_of_the_sick#Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Anointing of the sick">last sacrament</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> Alexei's tutor, <a href="/wiki/Pierre_Gilliard" title="Pierre Gilliard">Pierre Gilliard</a>, wrote to his children, "Pray, my children, pray daily and fervently for our precious heir."<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> On 10 October, a medical bulletin announcing Alexei's impending death was published in the newspapers.
</p><p>On 9 October, Alexandra asked her <a href="/wiki/Lady-in-waiting_of_the_Imperial_Court_of_Russia" title="Lady-in-waiting of the Imperial Court of Russia">lady-in-waiting</a> and best friend, <a href="/wiki/Anna_Vyrubova" title="Anna Vyrubova">Anna Vyrubova</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> to secure the help of Rasputin. According to his daughter, Rasputin received the telegram on 12 October.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;note 2&#93;</a></sup> On 9 October, Alexandra received a short telegram from Rasputin: "The little one will not die. Do not allow the doctors [c.q. <a href="/wiki/Eugene_Botkin" title="Eugene Botkin">Eugene Botkin</a> and <a href="/wiki/Vladimir_Derevenko" title="Vladimir Derevenko">Vladimir Derevenko</a>] to bother him too much."<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> Alexei's temperature dropped, and he began to improve. According to General Mosolov, the doctors "seemed in utter consternation"<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> at Alexei's sudden recovery. On 10 October, Doctor <a href="/wiki/Eugene_Botkin" title="Eugene Botkin">Eugene Botkin</a> wrote to his children that "our priceless patient" was "undoubtedly significantly better."<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> The positive trend continued throughout the next day.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> On 19 October Alexei's condition was much better and the hematoma disappeared. The boy had to undergo orthopedic therapy to straighten his left leg.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> Feodorov confirmed that Alexei's recovery was "wholly inexplicable, from a medical point of view."<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to Pierre Gilliard's 1921 memoir,
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>The Tsar had resisted the influence of Rasputin for a long time. At the beginning he had tolerated him because he dare not weaken the Tsarina's faith in him – a faith which kept her alive. He did not like to send him away for, if Alexei Nicolaievich had died, in the eyes of the mother he would have been the murderer of his own son.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>Observers and scholars have offered suggestions for Rasputin's apparent positive effect on Alexei: he used <a href="/wiki/Hypnotism" class="mw-redirect" title="Hypnotism">hypnotism</a>, administered herbs to the boy, or his advice to prevent too much action by the doctors aided the boy's healing. Others speculated that, with the information he got from his confidante at the court, lady-in-waiting <a href="/wiki/Anna_Vyrubova" title="Anna Vyrubova">Anna Vyrubova</a>, Rasputin timed his "interventions" for times when Alexei was already recovering, and claimed all the credit. Court physician Botkin believed that Rasputin was a charlatan and that his apparent healing powers were based on the use of <a href="/wiki/Hypnosis" title="Hypnosis">hypnosis</a> but Rasputin did not become interested in this practice before 1913 and his teacher Gerasim Papandato was expelled from St. Petersburg.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Felix_Yusupov" title="Felix Yusupov">Felix Yusupov</a>, one of Rasputin's enemies, suggested that he secretly gave Alexei <a href="/wiki/Traditional_Tibetan_medicine" title="Traditional Tibetan medicine">Tibetan herbs</a> which he got from <a href="/wiki/Quack_doctor" class="mw-redirect" title="Quack doctor">quack doctor</a> <a href="/wiki/Peter_Badmayev" title="Peter Badmayev">Peter Badmayev</a>, but these drugs were rejected by the court.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Maria_Rasputin" title="Maria Rasputin">Maria Rasputin</a> believed her father exercised <a href="/wiki/Animal_magnetism" title="Animal magnetism">magnetism</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Writers from the 1920s had a variety of explanations for the apparent effects of Rasputin. <a href="/wiki/Greg_King_(author)" title="Greg King (author)">Greg King</a> thinks such explanations fail to take into account those times when Rasputin apparently healed the boy, despite being 2600&#160;km (1650 miles) away. For historian Fuhrmann, these ideas on hypnosis and drugs flourished because the Imperial Family lived in such isolation from the wider world.<sup id="cite_ref-foreignaffairs.com_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-foreignaffairs.com-80">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> ("They lived almost as much apart from Russian society as if they were settlers in Canada."<sup id="cite_ref-foreignaffairs.com_80-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-foreignaffairs.com-80">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>) Moynahan says, "There is no evidence that Rasputin ever summoned up spirits, or felt the need to; he won his admirers through force of personality, not by tricks."<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>  Shelley said that the secret of Rasputin's power lay in the sense of calm, gentle strength, and shining warmth of conviction.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> Radzinsky wrote in 2000 that Rasputin believed he truly possessed a supernatural healing ability or that his prayers to God saved the boy.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Gilliard,<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> the French historian <a href="/wiki/H%C3%A9l%C3%A8ne_Carr%C3%A8re_d%27Encausse" title="Hélène Carrère d&#39;Encausse">Hélène Carrère d'Encausse</a><sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> and Diarmuid Jeffreys, a journalist, speculated that Rasputin may have halted the administration of <a href="/wiki/Aspirin" title="Aspirin">aspirin</a>. This pain-relieving <a href="/wiki/Analgesic" title="Analgesic">analgesic</a> had been available since 1899 but would have worsened Alexei's condition.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> Because <a href="/wiki/Mechanism_of_action_of_aspirin" title="Mechanism of action of aspirin">aspirin</a> is an <a href="/wiki/Antiaggregant" class="mw-redirect" title="Antiaggregant">antiaggregant</a> and has blood-thinning properties; it prevents <a href="/wiki/Coagulation" title="Coagulation">clotting</a>, and promotes <a href="/wiki/Bleeding" title="Bleeding">bleeding</a> which could have caused the <a href="/wiki/Hemarthrosis" title="Hemarthrosis">hemarthrosis</a>. The "<a href="/wiki/Non-steroidal_anti-inflammatory_drug" class="mw-redirect" title="Non-steroidal anti-inflammatory drug">anti-inflammatory drug</a>" would have worsened Alexei's joints' swelling and pain.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> According to historian M. Nelipa, <a href="/wiki/Robert_K._Massie" title="Robert K. Massie">Robert K. Massie</a> was correct to suggest that psychological factors play a part in the course of the disease.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Childhood">Childhood</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexei_Nikolaevich,_Tsarevich_of_Russia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Childhood">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Alexei_Nikolaievich_of_Russia_on_horseback.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/17/Alexei_Nikolaievich_of_Russia_on_horseback.jpg/220px-Alexei_Nikolaievich_of_Russia_on_horseback.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="171" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/17/Alexei_Nikolaievich_of_Russia_on_horseback.jpg/330px-Alexei_Nikolaievich_of_Russia_on_horseback.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/17/Alexei_Nikolaievich_of_Russia_on_horseback.jpg/440px-Alexei_Nikolaievich_of_Russia_on_horseback.jpg 2x" data-file-width="700" data-file-height="544" /></a><figcaption>Alexei on horseback in the uniform of the Nijni Novgorod Dragoons. In the background parts of the Alexander Palace can be seen. 1911</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tsarevich_Alexei_Nikolaevich.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3e/Tsarevich_Alexei_Nikolaevich.jpg/220px-Tsarevich_Alexei_Nikolaevich.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="413" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3e/Tsarevich_Alexei_Nikolaevich.jpg/330px-Tsarevich_Alexei_Nikolaevich.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3e/Tsarevich_Alexei_Nikolaevich.jpg/440px-Tsarevich_Alexei_Nikolaevich.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1764" data-file-height="3313" /></a><figcaption>Alexei in uniform of the Jaeger regiment of the Imperial family</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:%D0%98%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BE%D0%B2,_%D0%9A%D0%BE%D0%BD%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B8%D0%BD_%D0%90%D0%BB%D0%B5%D0%BA%D1%81%D0%B5%D0%B5%D0%B2%D0%B8%D1%87.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/%D0%98%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BE%D0%B2%2C_%D0%9A%D0%BE%D0%BD%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B8%D0%BD_%D0%90%D0%BB%D0%B5%D0%BA%D1%81%D0%B5%D0%B5%D0%B2%D0%B8%D1%87.png/220px-%D0%98%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BE%D0%B2%2C_%D0%9A%D0%BE%D0%BD%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B8%D0%BD_%D0%90%D0%BB%D0%B5%D0%BA%D1%81%D0%B5%D0%B5%D0%B2%D0%B8%D1%87.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="219" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/%D0%98%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BE%D0%B2%2C_%D0%9A%D0%BE%D0%BD%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B8%D0%BD_%D0%90%D0%BB%D0%B5%D0%BA%D1%81%D0%B5%D0%B5%D0%B2%D0%B8%D1%87.png/330px-%D0%98%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BE%D0%B2%2C_%D0%9A%D0%BE%D0%BD%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B8%D0%BD_%D0%90%D0%BB%D0%B5%D0%BA%D1%81%D0%B5%D0%B5%D0%B2%D0%B8%D1%87.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/%D0%98%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BE%D0%B2%2C_%D0%9A%D0%BE%D0%BD%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B8%D0%BD_%D0%90%D0%BB%D0%B5%D0%BA%D1%81%D0%B5%D0%B5%D0%B2%D0%B8%D1%87.png/440px-%D0%98%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BE%D0%B2%2C_%D0%9A%D0%BE%D0%BD%D1%81%D1%82%D0%B0%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B8%D0%BD_%D0%90%D0%BB%D0%B5%D0%BA%D1%81%D0%B5%D0%B5%D0%B2%D0%B8%D1%87.png 2x" data-file-width="877" data-file-height="874" /></a><figcaption>Standing left to right: footman Zhuravski, <a href="/wiki/Terenty_Ivanovich_Chemodurov" title="Terenty Ivanovich Chemodurov">Terenty Ivanovich Chemodurov</a>, Vasiliev, Petrov, <a href="/wiki/Pierre_Gilliard" title="Pierre Gilliard">Pierre Gilliard</a>, <a href="/wiki/Charles_Sydney_Gibbes" class="mw-redirect" title="Charles Sydney Gibbes">Charles Sydney Gibbes</a>. Second line: <a href="/wiki/Vladimir_Derevenko" title="Vladimir Derevenko">Vladimir Derevenko</a>, <a href="/wiki/Elizaveta_Ersberg" title="Elizaveta Ersberg">Elizaveta Ersberg</a>, <a href="/wiki/Alexandra_Tegleva" title="Alexandra Tegleva">Alexandra Tegleva</a>, Tsarevich Alexei Nikolaevich, <a href="/wiki/Maria_Gustavna_Tutelberg" title="Maria Gustavna Tutelberg">Maria Gustavna Tutelberg</a>, Kolya Derevenko, Alexei Derevenko, Alexandr Derevenko, and Sergei Derevenko; <a href="/wiki/Tsarskoe_Selo" class="mw-redirect" title="Tsarskoe Selo">Tsarskoe Selo</a> in 1916.</figcaption></figure>
<p>During <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>, he lived with his father at army headquarters in Mogilev for long stretches of time and observed military life.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> Alexei became one of the first <a href="/wiki/Boy_Scout" class="mw-redirect" title="Boy Scout">Boy Scouts</a> in Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-Pravoveriye_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pravoveriye-94">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In December 1916, <a href="/wiki/Major-General_(United_Kingdom)" class="mw-redirect" title="Major-General (United Kingdom)">Major-General</a> <a href="/wiki/John_Hanbury-Williams" title="John Hanbury-Williams">Sir John Hanbury-Williams</a>, head of the British military at <a href="/wiki/Stavka" title="Stavka">Stavka</a>, received word of the death of his son in action with the <a href="/wiki/British_Expeditionary_Force_(World_War_I)" title="British Expeditionary Force (World War I)">British Expeditionary Force</a> in France. Tsar Nicholas sent twelve-year-old Alexei to sit with the grieving father. "Papa told me to come sit with you as he thought you might feel lonely tonight," Alexei told the general.<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> Alexei, like all the Romanov men, grew up wearing sailor uniforms and playing at war from the time he was a toddler. His father began to prepare him for his future role as Tsar by inviting Alexei to sit in on long meetings with government ministers.<sup id="cite_ref-Massie,_pp._136-146_30-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Massie,_pp._136-146-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Stavka">Stavka</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexei_Nikolaevich,_Tsarevich_of_Russia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Stavka">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tsarevich_Alexei_by_anonymous_(1915-7,_Hillwood_museum).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0a/Tsarevich_Alexei_by_anonymous_%281915-7%2C_Hillwood_museum%29.jpg/220px-Tsarevich_Alexei_by_anonymous_%281915-7%2C_Hillwood_museum%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="292" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0a/Tsarevich_Alexei_by_anonymous_%281915-7%2C_Hillwood_museum%29.jpg/330px-Tsarevich_Alexei_by_anonymous_%281915-7%2C_Hillwood_museum%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0a/Tsarevich_Alexei_by_anonymous_%281915-7%2C_Hillwood_museum%29.jpg/440px-Tsarevich_Alexei_by_anonymous_%281915-7%2C_Hillwood_museum%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1508" data-file-height="2000" /></a><figcaption>Portrait of Alexei, dressed in the soldier's coat, cap and shoulder-boards of a corporal</figcaption></figure>
<p>During <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>, Alexei joined his father at <a href="/wiki/Stavka" title="Stavka">Stavka</a>, when his father became the <a href="/wiki/Commander-in-Chief" class="mw-redirect" title="Commander-in-Chief">Commander-in-Chief</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Imperial_Russian_Army" title="Imperial Russian Army">Russian Army</a> in 1915. Alexei seemed to like military life very much and became very playful and energetic. In one of his father's notes to his mother, he said "[I] have come in from the garden with wet sleeves and boots as Alexei sprayed us at the <a href="/wiki/Fountain" title="Fountain">fountain</a>. It is his favorite game [...] peals of laughter ring out. I keep an eye, in order to see that things do not go too far." Alexei even ate the soldiers' black bread, and refused when he was offered a meal that he would eat in his palace, saying "It's not what soldiers eat". In December 1915, Rasputin was invited to see Alexei when the 11-year-old boy was accidentally thrown against the window of a train and his nose began to bleed.
</p><p>In 1916, he was given the title of <a href="/wiki/Lance_Corporal" class="mw-redirect" title="Lance Corporal">Lance Corporal</a>, of which he was very proud. Alexei's favorites were the foreigners of Belgium, Britain, France, Japan, Italy, and Serbia, and in favor, adopted him as their mascot. <a href="/wiki/John_Hanbury-Williams" title="John Hanbury-Williams">Hanbury-Williams</a>, whom Alexei liked, wrote: "As time went on and his shyness wore off he treated us like old friends and [...] had always some bit fun with us. With me it was to make sure that each button on my coat was properly fastened, a habit which naturally made me take great care to have one or two unbuttoned, in which case he used to at once to stop and tell me that I was 'untidy again,' give a sigh at my lack of attention to these details and stop and carefully button me up again."
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Imprisonment_of_the_Imperial_family">Imprisonment of the Imperial family</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexei_Nikolaevich,_Tsarevich_of_Russia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Imprisonment of the Imperial family">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:AlexeiNicholas1917.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/AlexeiNicholas1917.jpg/220px-AlexeiNicholas1917.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="163" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/AlexeiNicholas1917.jpg/330px-AlexeiNicholas1917.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/AlexeiNicholas1917.jpg/440px-AlexeiNicholas1917.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1368" data-file-height="1016" /></a><figcaption>Nicholas and Alexei sawing wood in captivity at <a href="/wiki/Tobolsk" title="Tobolsk">Tobolsk</a> during the winter of 1917</figcaption></figure>
<p>The imperial family was arrested following the <a href="/wiki/February_Revolution" title="February Revolution">February Revolution</a> of 1917, which resulted in the <a href="/wiki/Abdication_of_Nicholas_II" title="Abdication of Nicholas II">abdication of Nicholas II</a>. When he was in captivity at <a href="/wiki/Tobolsk" title="Tobolsk">Tobolsk</a>, Alexei complained in his diary that he was "bored" and begged God to have "mercy" on him. He was permitted to play occasionally with Kolya, the son of one of his doctors, and with a kitchen boy named <a href="/wiki/Leonid_Sednev" title="Leonid Sednev">Leonid Sednev</a>. As he became older, Alexei seemed to tempt fate and injure himself on purpose. While in <a href="/wiki/Siberia" title="Siberia">Siberia</a>, he rode a sled down the stairs of the prison house and injured himself in the groin. The hemorrhage was very bad, and he was so ill that he could not be moved immediately when the Bolsheviks relocated his parents and older sister Maria to <a href="/wiki/Yekaterinburg" title="Yekaterinburg">Yekaterinburg</a> in April 1918. However, neither Nicholas II nor Empress Alexandra mention anything about a sledding accident in their diaries, and in fact primary resources such as letters of Empress Alexandra and the diaries of both Nicholas II and Alexandra state that the haemorrhage was caused by a coughing fit.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> On 30 March (12 April) 1918, Empress Alexandra recorded in her diary: 'Baby stays in bed as fr[om] coughing so hard has a slight haemorrhage in the abdom[en].<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> Every day from then onwards until her removal to Yekaterinburg, Alexandra recorded Alexei's condition in her diary. Alexei and his three remaining sisters joined the rest of the family weeks later.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> He was reliant on a wheelchair for the remaining weeks of his life.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Death">Death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexei_Nikolaevich,_Tsarevich_of_Russia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Murder_of_the_Romanov_family" title="Murder of the Romanov family">Murder of the Romanov family</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tsarevich_Alexei_and_Grand_Duchess_Tatiana_in_the_Park_at_Tsarskoye_Selo_in_1917.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/90/Tsarevich_Alexei_and_Grand_Duchess_Tatiana_in_the_Park_at_Tsarskoye_Selo_in_1917.jpg/220px-Tsarevich_Alexei_and_Grand_Duchess_Tatiana_in_the_Park_at_Tsarskoye_Selo_in_1917.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/90/Tsarevich_Alexei_and_Grand_Duchess_Tatiana_in_the_Park_at_Tsarskoye_Selo_in_1917.jpg/330px-Tsarevich_Alexei_and_Grand_Duchess_Tatiana_in_the_Park_at_Tsarskoye_Selo_in_1917.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/90/Tsarevich_Alexei_and_Grand_Duchess_Tatiana_in_the_Park_at_Tsarskoye_Selo_in_1917.jpg/440px-Tsarevich_Alexei_and_Grand_Duchess_Tatiana_in_the_Park_at_Tsarskoye_Selo_in_1917.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2592" data-file-height="1944" /></a><figcaption>Tsesarevich Alexei in his army uniform with sister <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchess_Tatiana_Nikolaevna_of_Russia" title="Grand Duchess Tatiana Nikolaevna of Russia">Tatiana</a> in 1917.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The Tsesarevich was murdered on 17 July 1918 aged 13 in the cellar room of the <a href="/wiki/Ipatiev_House" title="Ipatiev House">Ipatiev House</a> in <a href="/wiki/Yekaterinburg" title="Yekaterinburg">Yekaterinburg</a>.  The killings were carried out by forces of the <a href="/wiki/Bolshevik" class="mw-redirect" title="Bolshevik">Bolshevik</a> secret police under <a href="/wiki/Yakov_Yurovsky" title="Yakov Yurovsky">Yakov Yurovsky</a>. According to one account of the execution, the family was told to get up and get dressed in the middle of the night because they were going to be moved. Nicholas II carried Alexei to the cellar room. His mother asked for chairs to be brought so that she and Alexei could sit down. When the family and their servants were settled, Yurovsky announced that they were to be executed. The firing squad first killed Nicholas, the Tsarina, and the two male servants. Alexei remained sitting in the chair, "terrified," before the assassins turned on him and shot at him repeatedly. The boy remained alive and the killers tried to stab him multiple times with bayonets. "Nothing seemed to work," wrote Yurovsky later. "Though injured, he continued to live." Unbeknownst to the killing squad, the Tsarevich's torso was protected by a shirt wrapped in precious gems that he wore beneath his tunic. Finally Yurovsky fired two shots into the boy's head, and he fell silent.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>For decades (until all the bodies were found and identified, <a href="#Discovery_and_identification_of_remains">see below</a>) conspiracy theorists suggested that one or more of the family somehow survived the slaughter. Several people claimed to be surviving members of the Romanov family following the assassinations. People who have pretended to be the Tsarevich include: Alexei Poutziato, Joseph Veres, <a href="/wiki/Heino_Tammet" class="mw-redirect" title="Heino Tammet">Heino Tammet</a>, <a href="/wiki/Michael_Goleniewski" title="Michael Goleniewski">Michael Goleniewski</a> and Vassili Filatov. However, scientists considered it extremely unlikely that he escaped death, due to his lifelong hemophilia.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Discovery_and_identification_of_remains">Discovery and identification of remains</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexei_Nikolaevich,_Tsarevich_of_Russia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Discovery and identification of remains">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:AlexeiOlgaRus.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/AlexeiOlgaRus.jpg/220px-AlexeiOlgaRus.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/AlexeiOlgaRus.jpg/330px-AlexeiOlgaRus.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/AlexeiOlgaRus.jpg/440px-AlexeiOlgaRus.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1914" data-file-height="1077" /></a><figcaption>The last known photo of Alexei and sister <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchess_Olga_Nikolaevna_of_Russia" title="Grand Duchess Olga Nikolaevna of Russia">Olga</a> aboard the steamship <i>Rus</i> that took them to <a href="/wiki/Yekaterinburg" title="Yekaterinburg">Yekaterinburg</a> in May 1918.</figcaption></figure>
<p>On 23 August 2007, a Russian archaeologist announced the discovery of two burned, partial skeletons at a bonfire site near Yekaterinburg that appeared to match the site described in <a href="/wiki/Yakov_Yurovsky" title="Yakov Yurovsky">Yurovsky</a>'s memoirs. The archaeologists said the bones are from a boy who was roughly between the ages of twelve and fifteen years at the time of his death and of a young woman who was roughly between the ages of fifteen and nineteen years old.<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup>
Anastasia was seventeen years, one month old at the time of the assassination, while Maria was nineteen years, one month old. Alexei was two weeks shy of his fourteenth birthday. Alexei's elder sisters, Olga and Tatiana, were twenty-two and twenty-one years old, respectively, at the time of the assassination. Along with the remains of the two bodies, archaeologists found "shards of a container of <a href="/wiki/Sulfuric_acid" title="Sulfuric acid">sulfuric acid</a>, nails, metal strips from a wooden box, and bullets of various caliber." The bones were found using metal detectors and metal rods as probes. Also, striped material was found that appeared to have been from a blue-and-white striped cloth; Alexei commonly wore a blue-and-white striped undershirt.
</p><p>On 30 April 2008, Russian forensic scientists announced that DNA testing had proven that the remains belong to the Tsarevich Alexei and the Grand Duchess Maria.<sup id="cite_ref-Eckel_103-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eckel-103">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> DNA information, made public in July 2008, that was obtained from the <a href="/wiki/Yekaterinburg" title="Yekaterinburg">Yekaterinburg</a> site and repeated independent testing by laboratories such as the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Massachusetts_Medical_School" class="mw-redirect" title="University of Massachusetts Medical School">University of Massachusetts Medical School</a> revealed that the final two missing Romanov remains were indeed authentic and that the entire Romanov family lived in the Ipatiev House. In March 2009, results of the DNA testing were published, confirming that the two bodies discovered in 2007 were those of Tsarevich Alexei and Grand Duchess Maria.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Sainthood">Sainthood</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexei_Nikolaevich,_Tsarevich_of_Russia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Sainthood">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Canonization_of_the_Romanovs" title="Canonization of the Romanovs">Canonization of the Romanovs</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above n" style="background-color:gold;"><div style="display:inline;" class="fn">Saint Alexei Romanov</div></th></tr><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-header" style="background-color:gold;">Saint, Tsesarevich and Passion bearer</th></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Venerated&#160;in</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox Church</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">Canonized</a></th><td class="infobox-data">1981 and 2000 by Russian Orthodox Church Abroad and the Russian Orthodox Church</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><span class="nowrap">Major <a href="/wiki/Shrine" title="Shrine">shrine</a></span></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Church_of_All_Saints,_Yekaterinburg" title="Church of All Saints, Yekaterinburg">Church on Blood</a>, Yekaterinburg, Russia</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints" title="Calendar of saints">Feast</a></th><td class="infobox-data">17 July</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>The Romanov family had been canonized as <a href="/wiki/Martyr" title="Martyr">holy martyrs</a> in 1981 by the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church_Abroad" class="mw-redirect" title="Russian Orthodox Church Abroad">Russian Orthodox Church Abroad</a>, which had developed among emigres after the Russian Revolution and the World Wars.  After the decline of the Soviet Union, the government of Russia agreed with the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church" title="Russian Orthodox Church">Russian Orthodox Church</a> to have the bodies of Tsar Nicholas II, Tsarina Alexandra, and three of their daughters (which were found at the execution site) interred at <a href="/wiki/Saints_Peter_and_Paul_Cathedral,_Saint_Petersburg" title="Saints Peter and Paul Cathedral, Saint Petersburg">St. Peter and Paul Cathedral</a> in St. Petersburg on 17 July 1998, eighty years after they were executed.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> In 2000, Alexei and his family were <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonized</a> as <a href="/wiki/Passion_bearer" title="Passion bearer">passion bearers</a> by the Russian Orthodox Church.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As noted, the remains of Alexei and Maria's (or Anastasia's) bodies were found in 2008. As of 2015, Alexei's remains had not yet been interred with the rest of his family, as the Russian Orthodox Church has requested more DNA testing to ensure that his identity was confirmed.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-halign-center" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Nicholas_II_and_children_with_Cossacks_of_the_Guard,_cropped.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4d/Nicholas_II_and_children_with_Cossacks_of_the_Guard%2C_cropped.jpg/400px-Nicholas_II_and_children_with_Cossacks_of_the_Guard%2C_cropped.jpg" decoding="async" width="400" height="243" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4d/Nicholas_II_and_children_with_Cossacks_of_the_Guard%2C_cropped.jpg/600px-Nicholas_II_and_children_with_Cossacks_of_the_Guard%2C_cropped.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4d/Nicholas_II_and_children_with_Cossacks_of_the_Guard%2C_cropped.jpg/800px-Nicholas_II_and_children_with_Cossacks_of_the_Guard%2C_cropped.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2040" data-file-height="1240" /></a><figcaption><div class="center" style="width:auto; margin-left:auto; margin-right:auto;">The Romanovs with <a href="/wiki/His_Majesty%27s_Own_Cossack_Escort" title="His Majesty&#39;s Own Cossack Escort">His Majesty's Own Cossack Escort</a> during World War I. From left to right, Grand Duchess Anastasia, Grand Duchess Olga, <a href="/wiki/Tsar_Nicholas_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Tsar Nicholas II">Tsar Nicholas II</a>, Tsesarevich Alexei, Grand Duchess Tatiana, and Grand Duchess <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchess_Maria_Nikolaevna_of_Russia" title="Grand Duchess Maria Nikolaevna of Russia">Maria</a>, with <a href="/wiki/Kuban_Cossacks" title="Kuban Cossacks">Kuban Cossacks</a></div></figcaption></figure>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Historical_significance">Historical significance</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Alexei_Nikolaevich,_Tsarevich_of_Russia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Historical significance">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output
